A grown-up-sized long-sleeved, ankle-length, drop-hood zip-front onesie, with a big attached hood, with open cuffs and bare hands, and footless, ending in plain ankle cuffs with bare feet, in stretch cotton-blend jersey, a real plain stretch suit and not a real garment, covered edge to edge in a bold flat-colour illustration with thick black outlines, designed to make the onesie look like the outfit of an angel. The outfit's design is wrapped around the entire suit as one continuous print from the collar to the ankles, wrapping around the sides to the back. The print shows a white robe with feathered wings, with a halo printed on the hood, and the sleeves end in plain cuffs with no hands printed. Turn it round and the back carries the rear of the outfit, with nothing left plain. There is no separate background, the print is cut to the onesie's shape. Every sleeve and every leg of the suit is a tube covered completely, all the way round, in the outfit's cloth for that limb (sleeve cloth, trouser, tights or leg fabric), so no grey, blue or coloured background shows on the arms, legs, sides or back. There are no printed arms, hands, fingers, legs or feet and no printed skin on the suit, except a small printed neckline detail where the outfit is open. Every part of the shirt, shoulders and sleeves included, shows the outfit's own colours and cloth texture in one uniform print. The print is cut exactly to the suit, neck, shoulders, armholes, sleeves and hem are all covered in the outfit's own fabric. The outline is exactly a onesie's, nothing sticks out past the suit's edges, and no coat tails, skirts, capes, extra sleeves or loose layers hang outside it. The uniform fills the whole straight-sided suit shape edge to edge, the same width at chest, waist and hem. The printed parts are flat ink on stretch jersey. It is not a drawing of a person standing in the middle of the suit, no body, no head, no limbs and no figure are pictured, only the outfit's cloth, trims and fastenings spread over the whole suit. Raised plush layers are sewn on over the print, the buttons in felt, the epaulettes in faux fur and the badges and patches in fleece, standing out from the surface. The print gives the whole outfit, and raised layers of soft fabric are sewn on over it for the collar, pockets, belt, buttons and badges, so the suit is half picture and half sculpture. A full angel costume, head to toe in a single suit, with a white robe with feathered wings, a halo (as the hood), a harp sewn or attached to the suit. Nothing of the outfit is missing and the legs of the suit show the lower garment. Novelty embellishments, a rainbow of rickrack, ribbon and braid trim, beaded fringe along the hem, tufted boucle accents and satin ribbon bows and piping. It reads as a joyful, exaggerated novelty onesie, stuffed with texture and detail.

The print shows a brass-buttoned waistcoat with goggles and gears, breeches, with a top hat with goggles printed on the hood, with no footwear shown because the suit is footless, and printed gloves on the attached gloves at the sleeve ends. The back of the suit shows the matching back of the outfit, printed edge to edge, from the collar or hood to the ankles or feet. There is no separate background, the print is cut to the onesie's shape. The print is applied to the suit's own pattern pieces, front, back, each sleeve and each leg are fully covered in the matching cloth of the outfit, with no leftover background colour between them. There are no printed arms, hands, fingers, legs or feet and no printed skin on the suit, except a small printed neckline detail where the outfit is open. Every part of the shirt, shoulders and sleeves included, shows the outfit's own colours and cloth texture in one uniform print. The print is cut exactly to the suit, neck, shoulders, armholes, sleeves and hem are all covered in the outfit's own fabric. The silhouette is a plain one-piece suit, with every part of the outfit printed inside its shape and none beyond it. The outfit is laid out on the suit's own rectangle shape, fully covering it from side seam to side seam. The printed parts are flat ink on stretch jersey. The hood carries only hood cloth, no face, hair, ears or head is printed on it, and no crowd, audience, stage, lights or scene appears anywhere on the suit, only the outfit's own cloth. The printed top of breeches reaching the hem and ending there. Raised plush layers are sewn on over the print, the medals and ribbons in velvet, the buttons in chenille and the cuffs in fleece, standing out from the surface. The print gives the whole outfit, and raised layers of soft fabric are sewn on over it for the collar, pockets, belt, buttons and badges, so the suit is half picture and half sculpture. A full steampunk inventor costume, head to toe in a single suit, with a brass-buttoned waistcoat with goggles and gears, breeches on the legs, a top hat with goggles (as the hood), boots (as the feet), a wrench sewn or attached to the suit. Nothing of the outfit is missing and the legs of the suit show the lower garment. Novelty embellishments, enamel-style pins and patches, corduroy ridged panels, quilted puffy panels on the chest and puffy padded shoulder pieces. It reads as a joyful, exaggerated novelty onesie, stuffed with texture and detail.

The silhouette is a plain one-piece suit, with every part of the outfit printed inside its shape and none beyond it. There are no printed arms, hands, fingers, legs or feet and no printed skin on the suit, except a small printed neckline detail where the outfit is open. Every part of the shirt, shoulders and sleeves included, shows the outfit's own colours and cloth texture in one uniform print. The print is cut exactly to the suit, neck, shoulders, armholes, sleeves and hem are all covered in the outfit's own fabric. The outline is exactly a onesie's, nothing sticks out past the suit's edges, and no coat tails, skirts, capes, extra sleeves or loose layers hang outside it. The outfit is laid out on the suit's own rectangle shape, fully covering it from side seam to side seam. The printed parts are flat ink on stretch jersey. Novelty embellishments, rows of sequins sewn in swirling patterns, a quilted belt-like waist band with a big buckle shape, soft chenille patches and fluffy sherpa patches at the elbows and knees. Everything is chosen to be fun to look at and tactile to feel, bumpy, fluffy, shiny and soft in turn.
An easy-fitting adult-sized long-sleeved, ankle-length, drop-hood zip-front onesie, with an attached hood, with built-in gloves at the sleeve ends, and footless, ending in plain ankle cuffs with bare feet, in stretch cotton-blend jersey, a real plain stretch suit and not a real garment, covered edge to edge in a retro screen-printed illustration with limited colours, designed to make the onesie look like the outfit of a wizard. No blank jersey shows, the printed outfit runs from the neckline down the arms and legs to the cuffs, and over the hood if there is one. The print shows a long star-patterned robe, with a pointed hat printed on the hood, with no footwear shown because the suit is footless, and printed gloves on the attached gloves at the sleeve ends. The back of the suit shows the matching back of the outfit, printed edge to edge, from the collar or hood to the ankles or feet. There is no separate background, the print is cut to the onesie's shape. The print is applied to the suit's own pattern pieces, front, back, each sleeve and each leg are fully covered in the matching cloth of the outfit, with no leftover background colour between them. Where the outfit leaves arms or legs bare, the suit itself is cut sleeveless, short-sleeved or short-legged at that point, so the wearer's own limbs show; no printed arm, leg, hand, foot or skin appears on the suit's fabric. Every part of the shirt, shoulders and sleeves included, shows the outfit's own colours and cloth texture in one uniform print. The outfit's cloth runs unbroken from the collar band across both shoulders and down both sleeves to their hems, so every part of the suit is outfit cloth. The silhouette is a plain one-piece suit, with every part of the outfit printed inside its shape and none beyond it. The outfit is stretched to the suit's own straight-sided shape and fills it completely, so it suits any body size. The printed parts are flat ink on stretch jersey. It is not a drawing of a person standing in the middle of the suit, no body, no head, no limbs and no figure are pictured, only the outfit's cloth, trims and fastenings spread over the whole suit. Raised plush layers are sewn on over the print, the belt and buckle in faux leather, the badges and patches in quilted padding and the collar and lapels in velvet, standing out from the surface. Printed and plush together, the outfit is printed all over, with padded appliqué parts standing out from the surface. The suit is the whole wizard outfit, not just a top, with a long star-patterned robe, a pointed hat (as the hood), boots (as the feet), a staff sewn or attached to the suit. It is shown in full, from the neckline down to the ankle cuffs. Novelty embellishments, fuzzy pom-pom fringe down the back, faux-fur cuffs and collar, glitter-flecked tulle layered at the hem and braided cord drawstrings with knotted ends. It is a novelty costume garment, made to be bright, playful, over-the-top and a pleasure to touch.
